{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"0637fe90-dbd9-4339-b924-f54ffad3429a","agentId":"a3e05c7c400899d02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Audit the kuso repo at /Users/sisle/code/work/kuso for a specific overloaded-field bug. Context: a KusoEnvironment's `spec.kind` is overloaded. It carries CHART semantics (kind=\"production\" means always-on/no-TTL; kind=\"preview\" means ephemeral). But an env-group CLONE (staging/custom) ALSO stamps spec.kind=\"production\" (server-go/internal/projects/env_groups.go:605, \"chart-side semantics: always-on env\") even though the env's REAL group is its `kuso.sislelabs.com/env` LABEL (staging/production/preview-pr-N) + the `kuso.sislelabs.com/env-group-kind` label (custom). So any code that reads spec.kind==\"production\" to mean \"this belongs to the production env-group / is the canonical prod node\" is BUGGY — it will mis-group staging clones as production.\n\nTwo confirmed buggy readers already found:\n- web/src/components/canvas/ProjectCanvas.tsx:172 and :783 (select a service's node/env by spec.kind===\"production\")\n- (for contrast) server-go/internal/http/handlers/kubernetes_env_metrics.go:175 and builds.go:92,105 also branch on kind==\"production\" — determine if these are CORRECT (chart/always-on semantics) or BUGGY (grouping semantics).\n\nYOUR TASK — READ-ONLY, no edits:\n1. Find EVERY reader of a KusoEnvironment's kind field across the repo: grep for `spec.kind`, `.Spec.Kind`, `Spec.Kind ==`, `spec.kind ===`, `kind === \"production\"`, `kind == \"production\"`, `\"production\"`, `\"preview\"` in server-go/, web/src/, operator/, cli/. \n2. For EACH reader, classify it as:\n   - CORRECT-CHART: legitimately wants \"is this env always-on / not-a-preview\" (TTL, scaledown, always-on) — spec.kind is the right field.\n   - BUGGY-GROUPING: wants \"does this env belong to the production env-GROUP / is it the canonical node\" — should read the `kuso.sislelabs.com/env` label instead (==\"production\"), NOT spec.kind.\n   - AMBIGUOUS: needs a human decision.\n3. Also check: how does the operator/helm chart consume spec.kind (operator/watches.yaml, operator/helm-charts/kusoenvironment/) — does changing a clone's spec.kind away from \"production\" risk altering always-on/TTL behavior? This determines whether the fix should be canvas-side (leave spec.kind alone) or clone-side.\n4. Report each reader as: file:line | current condition | classification | why | suggested fix (if buggy). Be precise with file:line and quote the actual condition. This is the input to a fix, so completeness matters — miss no reader.\n\nDo NOT edit anything. Return the full classified list."},"uuid":"787f0f7b-3cc4-4457-818b-bd48a6e4af54","timestamp":"2026-07-24T14:22:13.533Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/sisle/code/work/kuso","sessionId":"57bf1edd-b71b-4f56-a06e-d7251af9b30a","version":"2.1.216","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"deb097f4-3660-4048-86db-8de32498b584","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a3e05c7c400899d02","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- analiz-analytics-interpretation: Use when the user asks to read, interpret, analyze, summarize, or act on Analiz analytics data via the mcp__analiz__* tools — covers \"how are we doing\", \"what's going on\", \"is feature X being used\", \"where's the leak\", \"should we be worried\", or any time analytics findings should be cross-referenced with the project's codebase, recent commits, or extended with custom events/funnels/revenue tracking.\n- setup-domain-email: Use when the user wants to set up, send, or receive email on a custom domain — e.g. \"send as me@mydomain.com\", \"set up email for mydomain.com\", \"add a Gmail Send-As for my domain\", \"make ivo@sislelabs.com work\". Covers the full path: Cloudflare Email Routing (receiving/forwarding) + Resend or Brevo (sending) + Gmail Send-As. Built around the mailctl CLI and its config at ~/.mailctl.yaml, but performs the underlying Cloudflare/Resend API calls directly when the CLI's interactive TUI can't run headless.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- feature-dev:feature-dev: Guided feature development with codebase understanding and architecture focus\n- code-review:code-review: Code review a pull request\n- claude-md-management:revise-claude-md: Update CLAUDE.md with learnings from this session\n- ralph-loop:cancel-ralph: Cancel active Ralph Loop\n- ralph-loop:help: Explain Ralph Loop plugin and available commands\n- ralph-loop:ralph-loop: Start Ralph Loop in current session\n- claude-enhanced:claw: Start NanoClaw v2 — ECC's persistent, zero-dependency REPL with model routing, skill hot-load, branching, compaction, export, and metrics.\n- claude-enhanced:e2e: Generate and run end-to-end tests with Playwright. Creates test journeys, runs tests, captures screenshots/videos/traces, and uploads artifacts.\n- claude-enhanced:evolve: Analyze instincts and suggest or generate evolved structures\n- claude-enhanced:go-build: Fix Go build errors, go vet warnings, and linter issues incrementally. Invokes the go-build-resolver agent for minimal, surgical fixes.\n- claude-enhanced:go-review: Comprehensive Go code review for idiomatic patterns, concurrency safety, error handling, and security. Invokes the go-reviewer agent.\n- claude-enhanced:go-test: Enforce TDD workflow for Go. Write table-driven tests first, then implement. Verify 80%+ coverage with go test -cover.\n- claude-enhanced:gradle-build: Fix Gradle build errors for Android and KMP projects\n- claude-enhanced:instinct-export: Export instincts from project/global scope to a file\n- claude-enhanced:instinct-import: Import instincts from file or URL into project/global scope\n- claude-enhanced:instinct-status: Show learned instincts (project + global) with confidence\n- claude-enhanced:learn-eval: Extract reusable patterns from the session, self-evaluate quality before saving, and determine the right save location (Global vs Project).\n- claude-enhanced:plan: Restate requirements, assess risks, and create step-by-step implementation plan. WAIT for user CONFIRM before touching any code.\n- claude-enhanced:projects: List known projects and their instinct statistics\n- claude-enhanced:promote: Promote project-scoped instincts to global scope\n- claude-enhanced:python-review: Comprehensive Python code review for PEP 8 compliance, type hints, security, and Pythonic idioms. Invokes the python-reviewer agent.\n- claude-enhanced:resume-session: Load the most recent session file from ~/.claude/sessions/ and resume work with full context from where the last session ended.\n- claude-enhanced:save-session: Save current session state to a dated file in ~/.claude/sessions/ so work can be resumed in a future session with full context.\n- claude-enhanced:skill-create: Analyze local git history to extract coding patterns and generate SKILL.md files. Local version of the Skill Creator GitHub App.\n- claude-enhanced:tdd: Enforce test-driven development workflow. Scaffold interfaces, generate tests FIRST, then implement minimal code to pass. Ensure 80%+ coverage.\n- stripe:explain-error: Explain Stripe error codes and provide solutions with code examples\n- stripe:test-cards: Display Stripe test card numbers for various testing scenarios\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver: Audit and improve CLAUDE.md files in repositories. Use when user asks to check, audit, update, improve, or fix CLAUDE.md files. Scans for all CLAUDE.md files, evaluates quality against templates, outputs quality report, then makes targeted updates. Also use when the user mentions \"CLAUDE.md maintenance\" or \"project memory optimization\".\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work - guides completion of development work by presenting structured options for merge, PR, or cleanup\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- claude-enhanced:agent-harness-construction: Design and optimize AI agent action spaces, tool definitions, and observation formatting for higher completion rates.\n- claude-enhanced:agentic-engineering: Operate as an agentic engineer using eval-first execution, decomposition, and cost-aware model routing.\n- claude-enhanced:ai-first-engineering: Engineering operating model for teams where AI agents generate a large share of implementation output.\n- claude-enhanced:android-clean-architecture: Clean Architecture patterns for Android and Kotlin Multiplatform projects — module structure, dependency rules, UseCases, Repositories, and data layer patterns.\n- claude-enhanced:api-design: REST API design patterns including resource naming, status codes, pagination, filtering, error responses, versioning, and rate limiting for production APIs.\n- claude-enhanced:article-writing: Write articles, guides, blog posts, tutorials, newsletter issues, and other long-form content in a distinctive voice derived from supplied examples or brand guidance. Use when the user wants polished written content longer than a paragraph, especially when voice consistency, structure, and credibility matter.\n- claude-enhanced:autonomous-loops: Patterns and architectures for autonomous Claude Code loops — from simple sequential pipelines to RFC-driven multi-agent DAG systems.\n- claude-enhanced:backend-patterns: Backend architecture patterns, API design, database optimization, and server-side best practices for Node.js, Express, and Next.js API routes.\n- claude-enhanced:blueprint: Turn a one-line objective into a step-by-step construction plan for multi-session, multi-agent engineering projects. Each step has a self-contained context brief so a fresh agent can execute it cold. Includes adversarial review gate, dependency graph, parallel step detection, anti-pattern catalog, and plan mutation protocol. TRIGGER when: user requests a plan, blueprint, or roadmap for a complex multi-PR task, or describes work that needs multiple sessions. DO NOT TRIGGER when: task is completable in a single PR or fewer than 3 tool calls, or user says \"just do it\".\n- claude-enhanced:carrier-relationship-management: Codified expertise for managing carrier portfolios, negotiating freight rates, tracking carrier performance, allocating freight, and maintaining strategic carrier relationships. Informed by transportation managers with 15+ years experience. Includes scorecarding frameworks, RFP processes, market intelligence, and compliance vetting. Use when the user wants social posts, threads, scripts, content calendars, or one source asset adapted cleanly across platforms.\n- claude-enhanced:content-hash-cache-pattern: Cache expensive file processing results using SHA-256 content hashes — path-independent, auto-invalidating, with service layer separation.\n- claude-enhanced:continuous-agent-loop: Patterns for continuous autonomous agent loops with quality gates, evals, and recovery controls.\n- claude-enhanced:continuous-learning: Automatically extract reusable patterns from Claude Code sessions and save them as learned skills for future use.\n- claude-enhanced:continuous-learning-v2: Instinct-based learning system that observes sessions via hooks, creates atomic instincts with confidence scoring, and evolves them into skills/commands/agents. v2.1 adds project-scoped instincts to prevent cross-project contamination.\n- claude-enhanced:cost-aware-llm-pipeline: Cost optimization patterns for LLM API usage — model routing by task complexity, budget tracking, retry logic, and prompt caching.\n- claude-enhanced:cpp-coding-standards: C++ coding standards based on the C++ Core Guidelines (isocpp.github.io). Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring C++ code to enforce modern, safe, and idiomatic practices.\n- claude-enhanced:cpp-testing: Use only when writing/updating/fixing C++ tests, configuring GoogleTest/CTest, diagnosing failing or flaky tests, or adding coverage/sanitizers.\n- claude-enhanced:customs-trade-compliance: Codified expertise for customs documentation, tariff classification, duty optimization, restricted party screening, and regulatory compliance across multiple jurisdictions. Informed by logistics professionals with 15+ years operational experience. Includes escalation protocols, carrier-specific behaviors, claims procedures, and judgment frameworks. Use when handling shipping exceptions, freight claims, delivery issues, or carrier disputes.\n- claude-enhanced:market-research: Conduct market research, competitive analysis, investor due diligence, and industry intelligence with source attribution and decision-oriented summaries. Use when the user wants market sizing, competitor comparisons, fund research, technology scans, or research that informs business decisions.\n- claude-enhanced:nanoclaw-repl: Operate and extend NanoClaw v2, ECC's zero-dependency session-aware REPL built on claude -p.\n- claude-enhanced:nutrient-document-processing: Process, convert, OCR, extract, redact, sign, and fill documents using the Nutrient DWS API. Works with PDFs, DOCX, XLSX, PPTX, HTML, and images.\n- claude-enhanced:perl-patterns: Modern Perl 5.36+ idioms, best practices, and conventions for building robust, maintainable Perl applications.\n- claude-enhanced:perl-security: Comprehensive Perl security covering taint mode, input validation, safe process execution, DBI parameterized queries, web security (XSS/SQLi/CSRF), and perlcritic security policies.\n- claude-enhanced:perl-testing: Perl testing patterns using Test2::V0, Test::More, prove runner, mocking, coverage with Devel::Cover, and TDD methodology.\n- claude-enhanced:plankton-code-quality: Write-time code quality enforcement using Plankton — auto-formatting, linting, and Claude-powered fixes on every file edit via hooks.\n- claude-enhanced:postgres-patterns: PostgreSQL database patterns for query optimization, schema design, indexing, and security. Based on Supabase best practices.\n- claude-enhanced:production-scheduling: Codified expertise for production scheduling, job sequencing, line balancing, changeover optimization, and bottleneck resolution in discrete and batch manufacturing. Informed by production schedulers with 15+ years experience. Includes TOC/drum-buffer-rope, SMED, OEE analysis, disruption response frameworks, and ERP/MES interaction patterns. Use when scheduling production, resolving bottlenecks, optimizing changeovers, responding to disruptions, or balancing manufacturing lines.\n- claude-enhanced:project-guidelines-example: Example project-specific skill template based on a real production application.\n- claude-enhanced:python-patterns: Pythonic idioms, PEP 8 standards, type hints, and best practices for building robust, efficient, and maintainable Python applications.\n- claude-enhanced:python-testing: Python testing strategies using pytest, TDD methodology, fixtures, mocking, parametrization, and coverage requirements.\n- claude-enhanced:quality-nonconformance: Codified expertise for quality control, non-conformance investigation, root cause analysis, corrective action, and supplier quality management in regulated manufacturing. Informed by quality engineers with 15+ years experience across FDA, IATF 16949, and AS9100 environments. Includes NCR lifecycle management, CAPA systems, SPC interpretation, and audit methodology. Use when investigating non-conformances, performing root cause analysis, managing CAPAs, interpreting SPC data, or handling supplier quality issues.\n- claude-enhanced:ralphinho-rfc-pipeline: RFC-driven multi-agent DAG execution pattern with quality gates, merge queues, and work unit orchestration.\n- claude-enhanced:regex-vs-llm-structured-text\n- claude-enhanced:returns-reverse-logistics\n- claude-enhanced:search-first\n- claude-enhanced:security-review: Use this skill when adding authentication, handling user input, working with secrets, creating API endpoints, or implementing payment/sensitive features. Provides comprehensive security checklist and patterns.\n- claude-enhanced:security-scan\n- claude-enhanced:skill-stocktake\n- claude-enhanced:springboot-patterns\n- claude-enhanced:springboot-security\n- claude-enhanced:springboot-tdd\n- claude-enhanced:springboot-verification\n- claude-enhanced:strategic-compact\n- claude-enhanced:swift-actor-persistence\n- claude-enhanced:swift-concurrency-6-2\n- claude-enhanced:swift-protocol-di-testing\n- claude-enhanced:swiftui-patterns\n- claude-enhanced:tdd-workflow\n- claude-enhanced:verification-loop\n- claude-enhanced:videodb\n- claude-enhanced:visa-doc-translate\n- stripe:connect-recommend\n- stripe:stripe-best-practices: Guides Stripe integration decisions across API selection (Checkout Sessions vs PaymentIntents), Connect platform setup (Accounts v2, controller properties), billing/subscriptions, tax and registrations (Stripe Tax, automatic_tax, product tax codes), Treasury financial accounts, integration options (Checkout, Payment Element), migrating from deprecated Stripe APIs, and security best practices (API key management, restricted keys, webhooks, OAuth). Use when building, modifying, or reviewing any Stripe integration, including accepting payments, building marketplaces, integrating Stripe, processing payments, setting up subscriptions, collecting sales tax, VAT, or GST, creating connected accounts, or implementing secure key handling.\n- stripe:stripe-directory\n- stripe:stripe-projects\n- stripe:upgrade-stripe: Guide for upgrading Stripe API versions and SDKs\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: The instance label on\n161\t\t// metrics-server pods matches the env CR name, so once we have the\n162\t\t// list we filter pod metrics by `app.kubernetes.io/instance in\n163\t\t// (env1, env2, ...)`.\n164\t\tenvs, err := h.Kube.ListKusoEnvironmentsByLabels(ctx, h.Namespace, map[string]string{\n165\t\t\t\"kuso.sislelabs.com/project\": project,\n166\t\t})\n167\t\tif err != nil {\n168\t\t\t// Treat list failure as \"no data\" — the card just shows \"—\"\n169\t\t\t// rather than an error banner.\n170\t\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, out)\n171\t\t\treturn\n172\t\t}\n173\t\tprodEnvs := make(map[string]struct{}, len(envs))\n174\t\tfor i := range envs {\n175\t\t\tif envs[i].Spec.Kind == \"production\" {\n176\t\t\t\tprodEnvs[envs[i].Name] = struct{}{}\n177\t\t\t}\n178\t\t}\n179\t\tout.Envs = len(prodEnvs)\n180\t\tif len(prodEnvs) == 0 {\n181\t\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, out)\n182\t\t\treturn\n183\t\t}\n184\t\t// Build a label-selector that matches any of the production env\n185\t\t// names. metrics.k8s.io supports the standard set-based selector.\n186\t\tenvNames := make([]string, 0, len(prodEnvs))\n187\t\tfor n := range prodEnvs {\n188\t\t\tenvNames = append(envNames, n)\n189\t\t}"}]},"uuid":"1fe495c2-d42e-40f5-aee7-27c255fd34c6","timestamp":"2026-07-24T14:22:30.320Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"server-go/internal/http/handlers/kubernetes_env_metrics.go","content":"\tPods     int    `json:\"pods\"`\n\tEnvs     int    `json:\"envs\"`\n}\n\n// ProjectMetrics returns CPU + memory summed across every production\n// KusoEnvironment in the project. Empty/zero results when:\n//   - the project has no production envs (Pods=0, Envs=0)\n//   - metrics-server is missing (graceful fall-through; Pods=0)\n//   - the user lacks viewer role on the project (403)\n//\n// We deliberately limit to kind=\"production\" so previews coming and\n// going don't make the card numbers jitter; the per-env panel under\n// /projects/<name> still shows preview env metrics individually.\nfunc (h *KubernetesHandler) ProjectMetrics(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tproject := chi.URLParam(r, \"project\")\n\tif project == \"\" {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"missing project\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tctx, cancel := kubeCtx(r)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\tif !requireProjectAccess(ctx, w, h.DB, project, db.ProjectRoleViewer) {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tout := projectMetricsResponse{Project: project}\n\tif h.Kube == nil {\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, out)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\t// Resolve project → production env CR names. The instance label on\n\t// metrics-server pods matches the env CR name, so once we have the\n\t// list we filter pod metrics by `app.kubernetes.io/instance in\n\t// (env1, env2, ...)`.\n\tenvs, err := h.Kube.ListKusoEnvironmentsByLabels(ctx, h.Namespace, map[string]string{\n\t\t\"kuso.sislelabs.com/project\": project,\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t// Treat list failure as \"no data\" — the card just shows \"—\"\n\t\t// rather than an error banner.\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, out)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tprodEnvs := make(map[string]struct{}, len(envs))\n\tfor i := range envs {\n\t\tif envs[i].Spec.Kind == \"production\" {\n\t\t\tprodEnvs[envs[i].Name] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tout.Envs = len(prodEnvs)\n\tif len(prodEnvs) == 0 {\n\t\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, out)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\t// Build a label-selector that matches any of the production env\n\t// names. metrics.k8s.io supports the standard set-based selector.\n\tenvNames := make([]string, 0, len(prodEnvs))\n\tfor n := range prodEnvs {\n\t\tenvNames = append(envNames, n)\n\t}","numLines":60,"startLine":130,"totalLines":420}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0028a59e-97f5-4506-9bc0-3e3a2877fe28","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/sisle/code/work/kuso","sessionId":"57bf1edd-b71b-4f56-a06e-d7251af9b30a","version":"2.1.216","gitBranch":"main"}

Cache: serviceFQN → predicate.\n\tserviceBranchAllowed := map[string]predFn{}\n\tresolveForService := func(serviceFQN string) predFn {\n\t\tif envFilter == \"\" || envFilter == \"production\" {\n\t\t\treturn branchAllowed\n\t\t}\n\t\tif cached, ok := serviceBranchAllowed[serviceFQN]; ok {\n\t\t\treturn cached\n\t\t}\n\t\tenvName := serviceFQN + \"-\" + envFilter\n\t\tenv, _ := h.Svc.Kube.GetKusoEnvironment(ctx, h.Svc.Namespace, envName)\n\t\tvar pred predFn\n\t\tif env != nil && env.Spec.Branch != \"\" {\n\t\t\texpect := env.Spec.Branch\n\t\t\tpred = func(b string) bool { return b == expect || b == \"\" }\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t// Env CR missing or branch unset — fall back to matching\n\t\t\t// the env name as the branch (staging env tracking the\n\t\t\t// \"staging\" branch is the common case).\n\t\t\texpect := envFilter\n\t\t\tpred = func(b string) bool { return b == expect || b == \"\" }\n\t\t}\n\t\tserviceBranchAllowed[serviceFQN] = pred\n\t\treturn pred\n\t}\n\t// Newest-first ordering already enforced by Service.List, so the\n\t// first matching build we see for a given service IS the latest.\n\tseen := map[string]bool{}\n\tout := map[string]buildSummary{}\n\tfor _, b := range raw {\n\t\tfq := b.Spec.Service\n\t\tshort := fq\n\t\tif prefix := project + \"-\"; len(fq) > len(prefix) && fq[:len(prefix)] == prefix {\n\t\t\tshort = fq[len(prefix):]\n\t\t}\n\t\tif seen[short] {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tpred := resolveForService(fq)\n\t\tif !pred(b.Spec.Branch) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tseen[short] = true\n\t\tout[short] = toBuildSummary(b)\n\t}\n\t// Backfill from the archive for services whose live CRs the 24h\n\t// retention sweep already deleted (mirrors the per-service List\n\t// handler). Without this the canvas loses the latest-build badge +\n\t// commit chip the morning after a deploy day and decays to \"env\n\t// created Nd ago\". Best-effort: an archive read failure still\n\t// returns the live results.\n\tif h.DB != nil {\n\t\trecords, rerr := h.DB.ListProjectBuildRecords(ctx, project, 0)\n\t\tif rerr != nil {\n\t\t\th.Logger.Warn(\"latest builds: archive read\", \"project\", project, \"err\", rerr)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tbackfillLatestFromArchive(out, records, project, func(serviceFQN, branch string) bool {\n\t\t\t\treturn resolveForService(serviceFQN)(branch)\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\twriteJSON(w, http.StatusOK, out)\n}\n\n// backfillLatestFromArchive fills services missing from `out` with their\n// newest branch-matching archived build. `records` must be newest-first\n// (ListProjectBuildRecords order); the first hit per service wins. A\n// service already in `out` (live CR) is never overwritten — the CR is\n// the fresher source. Record.Service is the short name, but tolerate a\n// project-prefixed form for robustness.\nfunc backfillLatestFromArchive(out map[string]buildSummary, records []db.BuildRecord, project string, branchAllowed func(serviceFQN, branch string) bool) {\n\tfor _, rec := range records {\n\t\tshort := rec.Service\n\t\tif prefix := project + \"-\"; len(short) > len(prefix) && short[:len(prefix)] == prefix {\n\t\t\tshort = short[len(prefix):]\n\t\t}\n\t\tif _, ok := out[short]; ok {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !branchAllowed(project+\"-\"+short, rec.Branch) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tout[short] = recordToSummary(rec)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (h *BuildsHandler) Cancel(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tctx, cancel := buildsCtx(r)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\tif !requireProjectAccess(ctx, w, h.DB, chi.URLParam(r, \"project\"), db.ProjectRoleEditor) {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\terr := h.Svc.Cancel(ctx, chi.URLParam(r, \"project\"), chi.URLParam(r, \"service\"), chi.URLParam(r, \"build\"))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\th.fail(w, \"cancel build\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)","numLines":120,"startLine":80,"totalLines":454}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"75218eeb-af5b-419f-800c-1e9beb85ab55","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/sisle/code/work/kuso","sessionId":"57bf1edd-b71b-4f56-a06e-d7251af9b30a","version":"2.1.216","gitBranch":"main"}

Each cloned KusoService has its\n541\t\t\t// own KusoEnvironment named after itself + \"-production\" —\n542\t\t\t// the same scheme AddService uses for the canonical prod env.\n543\t\t\t// Using newSvcCR alone collided with the KusoService release\n544\t\t\t// (helm-operator: \"duplicate release name … for chart\n545\t\t\t// kusoservice\"). Adding the \"-production\" suffix\n546\t\t\t// disambiguates while keeping the chart-side semantics\n547\t\t\t// (Kind: \"production\") identical.\n548\t\t\tenvCRName := newSvcCR + \"-production\"\n549\t\t\thost := buildEnvHost(proj.Spec.BaseDomain, project, item.short, req.Name)\n550\t\n551\t\t\t// Seed the cloned env with production's currently-deployed\n552\t\t\t// image so the helm-operator can render a working Deployment\n553\t\t\t// immediately. Without this, spec.image is empty → chart\n554\t\t\t// renders \":latest\" which kube can't pull → pods sit at\n555\t\t\t// InvalidImageName forever, requiring a manual Redeploy. The\n556\t\t\t// production env CR is named \"<project>-<service>-production\"\n557\t\t\t// (see projects.productionEnvName) — NOT just <fqn>.\n558\t\t\tvar inheritImage *kube.KusoImage\n559\t\t\tprodEnvCRName := fmt.Sprintf(\"%s-production\", item.fqn)\n560\t\t\tif prodEnv, perr := s.Kube.GetKusoEnvironment(ctx, ns, prodEnvCRName); err == nil {\n579\t\t\t\t\tannot[envGroupAddonPolicyAnnotation] = string(raw)\n580\t\t\t\t}\n581\t\t\t}\n582\t\t\tenvCR := &kube.KusoEnvironment{\n583\t\t\t\tObjectMeta: metav1.ObjectMeta{\n584\t\t\t\t\tName: envCRName,\n585\t\t\t\t\tLabels: map[string]string{\n586\t\t\t\t\t\tlabelProject: project,\n587\t\t\t\t\t\t// SHORT service name — matches the production-env\n588\t\t\t\t\t\t// convention (services_ops.go). propagateChangedToEnvs\n589\t\t\t\t\t\t// lists envs by labelService=<short>, so labeling this with\n590\t\t\t\t\t\t// the FQN (newSvcCR) silently hid the cloned env from every\n591\t\t\t\t\t\t// propagation, freezing its env-vars/scale/etc.\n592\t\t\t\t\t\tlabelService: newSvcShort,\n593\t\t\t\t\t\tlabelEnv:     req.Name,\n594\t\t\t\t\t},\n595\t\t\t\t\tAnnotations: annot,\n596\t\t\t\t\t// Own the env by the CLONE, not the source production service.\n597\t\t\t\t\t// Owning it by item.svc meant deleting production GC'd this\n598\t\t\t\t\t// clone's env (cross-env hazard) while deleting the clone did\n599\t\t\t\t\t// not (orphan). createdClone carries the UID from Create.\n600\t\t\t\t\tOwnerReferences: []metav1.OwnerReference{kube.OwnerRefForService(createdClone)},\n601\t\t\t\t},\n602\t\t\t\tSpec: kube.KusoEnvironmentSpec{\n603\t\t\t\t\tProject:      project,\n604\t\t\t\t\tService:      newSvcCR,\n605\t\t\t\t\tKind:         \"production\", // chart-side semantics: \"always-on env\"\n606\t\t\t\t\tBranch:       coalesce(item.svc.Spec.Repo).DefaultBranch,\n607\t\t\t\t\tPort:         port,\n608\t\t\t\t\tReplicaCount: intPtr(scaleMin),\n609\t\t\t\t\tAutoscaling:  autoscalingFromScale(item.svc.Spec.Scale),\n610\t\t\t\t\tHost:         host,\n611\t\t\t\t\t// Do NOT inherit the source service's custom domains into the\n612\t\t\t\t\t// clone. Stamping item.svc.Spec.Domains here made the cloned\n613\t\t\t\t\t// env claim production's AdditionalHosts/TLSHosts with TLS on —\n614\t\t\t\t\t// its Ingress then races production for the same host and Let's\n615\t\t\t\t\t// Encrypt cert (traffic hijack + rate-limit burn). err == nil {\n\t\t\t\tannot[envGroupAddonPolicyAnnotation] = string(raw)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tenvCR := &kube.KusoEnvironment{\n\t\t\tObjectMeta: metav1.ObjectMeta{\n\t\t\t\tName: envCRName,\n\t\t\t\tLabels: map[string]string{\n\t\t\t\t\tlabelProject: project,\n\t\t\t\t\t// SHORT service name — matches the production-env\n\t\t\t\t\t// convention (services_ops.go). propagateChangedToEnvs\n\t\t\t\t\t// lists envs by labelService=<short>, so labeling this with\n\t\t\t\t\t// the FQN (newSvcCR) silently hid the cloned env from every\n\t\t\t\t\t// propagation, freezing its env-vars/scale/etc.\n\t\t\t\t\tlabelService: newSvcShort,\n\t\t\t\t\tlabelEnv:     req.Name,\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t\tAnnotations: annot,\n\t\t\t\t// Own the env by the CLONE, not the source production service.\n\t\t\t\t// Owning it by item.svc meant deleting production GC'd this\n\t\t\t\t// clone's env (cross-env hazard) while deleting the clone did\n\t\t\t\t// not (orphan). createdClone carries the UID from Create.\n\t\t\t\tOwnerReferences: []metav1.OwnerReference{kube.OwnerRefForService(createdClone)},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tSpec: kube.KusoEnvironmentSpec{\n\t\t\t\tProject:      project,\n\t\t\t\tService:      newSvcCR,\n\t\t\t\tKind:         \"production\", // chart-side semantics: \"always-on env\"\n\t\t\t\tBranch:       coalesce(item.svc.Spec.Repo).DefaultBranch,\n\t\t\t\tPort:         port,\n\t\t\t\tReplicaCount: intPtr(scaleMin),\n\t\t\t\tAutoscaling:  autoscalingFromScale(item.svc.Spec.Scale),\n\t\t\t\tHost:         host,\n\t\t\t\t// Do NOT inherit the source service's custom domains into the\n\t\t\t\t// clone. Stamping item.svc.Spec.Domains here made the cloned\n\t\t\t\t// env claim production's AdditionalHosts/TLSHosts with TLS on —\n\t\t\t\t// its Ingress then races production for the same host and Let's\n\t\t\t\t// Encrypt cert (traffic hijack + rate-limit burn). The\n\t\t\t\t// single-env / preview path NILs these for the same reason\n\t\t\t\t// (services_ops.go ~L987). Custom domains on a cloned env are\n\t\t\t\t// an explicit opt-in via `kuso domains add` after creation.\n\t\t\t\tAdditionalHosts:  nil,","numLines":80,"startLine":540,"totalLines":1082}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"11d3081c-5d3d-4d15-9159-2266a095046a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/sisle/code/work/kuso","sessionId":"57bf1edd-b71b-4f56-a06e-d7251af9b30a","version":"2.1.216","gitBranch":"main"}

The previous\n418\t  // split(\"-\").slice(-2) heuristic produced \"frontend-staging\" for\n419\t  // an env CR named \"tickero-frontend-staging\" — comparing that\n420\t  // against envParam=\"staging\" returned no match, so the overlay\n421\t  // fell back to the service-level domains list (production URL)\n422\t  // on every non-production tab. []).find((e) => {\n426\t    if (e.spec.service !== fqn) return false;\n427\t    if (envParam === \"production\") return e.spec.kind === \"production\";\n428\t    const envLabel = (e.metadata.labels ?? {})[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"];\n429\t    if (envLabel) return envLabel === envParam;\n430\t    // Fallback: last segment of the CR name (tickero-frontend-staging → staging).\n431\t    const parts = e.metadata.name.split(\"-\");\n432\t    return parts[parts.length - 1] === envParam;\n433\t  });\n434\t\n435\t  // URL fallback chain — mirrors ServiceNode.tsx so the overlay\n436\t  // header and the canvas chip agree.\n437\t  //\n438\t  // Pre-fix this read only env.status.url which the operator never\n439\t  // populates today, so every overlay header said \"no URL yet\" even"}]},"uuid":"51184147-a0c6-4485-a5ba-bc27b29f6663","timestamp":"2026-07-24T14:23:22.192Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"web/src/components/service/ServiceOverlay.tsx","content":"  }, [tab, showCrons, showRuns, canShell]);\n  const fqn = service ? project + \"-\" + service : \"\";\n  // Find the env CR matching (service, envParam). The previous\n  // split(\"-\").slice(-2) heuristic produced \"frontend-staging\" for\n  // an env CR named \"tickero-frontend-staging\" — comparing that\n  // against envParam=\"staging\" returned no match, so the overlay\n  // fell back to the service-level domains list (production URL)\n  // on every non-production tab. Use the kuso.sislelabs.com/env\n  // label which is the canonical env short-name, with a single-\n  // segment fallback for legacy CRs without the label.\n  const env = (envs.data ?? []).find((e) => {\n    if (e.spec.service !== fqn) return false;\n    if (envParam === \"production\") return e.spec.kind === \"production\";\n    const envLabel = (e.metadata.labels ?? {})[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"];\n    if (envLabel) return envLabel === envParam;\n    // Fallback: last segment of the CR name (tickero-frontend-staging → staging).\n    const parts = e.metadata.name.split(\"-\");\n    return parts[parts.length - 1] === envParam;\n  });\n\n  // URL fallback chain — mirrors ServiceNode.tsx so the overlay\n  // header and the canvas chip agree.\n  //\n  // Pre-fix this read only env.status.url which the operator never\n  // populates today, so every overlay header said \"no URL yet\" even","numLines":25,"startLine":415,"totalLines":1239}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f3a679fe-10ac-4b61-ac1a-41f7d0449dd8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/sisle/code/work/kuso","sessionId":"57bf1edd-b71b-4f56-a06e-d7251af9b30a","version":"2.1.216","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"a237f968-6bea-4ab0-90e6-f5595b308134","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"0637fe90-dbd9-4339-b924-f54ffad3429a","agentId":"a3e05c7c400899d02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01D4ZRVdF96a8RxnxEd5g5FV","type":"tool_result","content":"35\t  // this service (production + any preview-pr-N). The dropdown used\n36\t  // to be hard-coded \"all envs / production\" so users couldn't scope\n37\t  // to a preview at all.\n38\t  const envs = useEnvironments(project);\n39\t  const fqn = service ? project + \"-\" + service : \"\";\n40\t  const envOptions = useMemo(() => {\n41\t    const list = (envs.data ?? []).filter((e) => e.spec.service === fqn);\n42\t    return list\n43\t      .map((e) => {\n44\t        if (e.spec.kind === \"production\") return { value: \"production\", label: \"production\" };\n45\t        // The log store filters by the pod's `kuso.sislelabs.com/env`\n46\t        // label (logship shipper.go:317), so the dropdown VALUE must equal\n47\t        // that label. Prefer the label off the env CR; fall back to\n48\t        // stripping the real `<project>-<service>-` prefix from the CR\n49\t        // name. The old `slice(-2)` was wrong for single-segment env ids\n50\t        // (env `staging` on service `frontend` → CR `frontend-staging` →\n51\t        // yielded `frontend-staging`, so the filter matched zero rows).\n52\t        const short =\n53\t          e.metadata.labels?.[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"] ||\n54\t          (fqn && e.metadata.name.startsWith(fqn + \"-\")\n55\t            ? e.metadata.name.slice(fqn.length + 1)\n56\t            : e.metadata.name);\n57\t        return { value: short, label: short };\n58\t      })\n59\t      .sort((a, b) => {\n60\t        if (a.value === \"production\") return -1;\n61\t        if (b.value === \"production\") return 1;\n62\t        return a.label.localeCompare(b.label);\n63\t      });\n64\t  }, [envs.data, fqn]);"}]},"uuid":"304dba35-f5d7-4161-b651-8e553426cd34","timestamp":"2026-07-24T14:23:23.434Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"web/src/components/service/overlay/ServiceLogsPanel.tsx","content":"  // this service (production + any preview-pr-N). []).filter((e) => e.spec.service === fqn);\n    return list\n      .map((e) => {\n        if (e.spec.kind === \"production\") return { value: \"production\", label: \"production\" };\n        // The log store filters by the pod's `kuso.sislelabs.com/env`\n        // label (logship shipper.go:317), so the dropdown VALUE must equal\n        // that label. Prefer the label off the env CR; fall back to\n        // stripping the real `<project>-<service>-` prefix from the CR\n        // name. The old `slice(-2)` was wrong for single-segment env ids\n        // (env `staging` on service `frontend` → CR `frontend-staging` →\n        // yielded `frontend-staging`, so the filter matched zero rows).\n        const short =\n          e.metadata.labels?.[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"] ||\n          (fqn && e.metadata.name.startsWith(fqn + \"-\")\n            ? e.metadata.name.slice(fqn.length + 1)\n            : e.metadata.name);\n        return { value: short, label: short };\n      })\n      .sort((a, b) => {\n        if (a.value === \"production\") return -1;\n        if (b.value === \"production\") return 1;\n        return a.label.localeCompare(b.label);\n      });\n  }, [envs.data, fqn]);","numLines":30,"startLine":35,"totalLines":318}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a237f968-6bea-4ab0-90e6-f5595b308134","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/sisle/code/work/kuso","sessionId":"57bf1edd-b71b-4f56-a06e-d7251af9b30a","version":"2.1.216","gitBranch":"main"}

If a preview needs vars, they're set per-env.\n463\tfunc (s *Service) attachToAllEnvs(ctx context.Context, project, service, secretName string) error {\n464\t\tenvs, err := s.envsForService(ctx, project, service)\n465\t\tif err != nil {\n466\t\t\treturn err\n467\t\t}\n468\t\tns := s.nsFor(ctx, project)\n469\t\tfor _, e := range envs {\n470\t\t\tif e.Spec.Kind == \"preview\" {\n471\t\t\t\tcontinue\n472\t\t\t}\n473\t\t\talreadyAttached := false\n474\t\t\tfor _, existing := range e.Spec.EnvFromSecrets {\n475\t\t\t\tif existing == secretName {\n476\t\t\t\t\talreadyAttached = true\n477\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n478\t\t\t\t}\n479\t\t\t}\n480\t\t\tif alreadyAttached {\n481\t\t\t\tcontinue\n482\t\t\t}\n483\t\t\tpatch := fmt.Sprintf(`{\"spec\":{\"envFromSecrets\":%s}}`, jsonStringList(append(e.Spec.EnvFromSecrets, secretName)))\n484\t\t\tif err := s.patchEnv(ctx, ns, e.Name, patch); err != nil {\n485\t\t\t\treturn err\n486\t\t\t}\n487\t\t}\n488\t\treturn nil\n489\t}\n490\t\n491\t// detachFromAllEnvs removes a shared secret from every NON-preview env\n492\t// of a service. Symmetric with attachToAllEnvs — both paths skip\n493\t// previews so the attach/detach surface stays consistent. In practice\n494\t// the skip is a no-op today (previews never had the shared secret\n495\t// attached, so the envFromSecrets diff would already be empty), but\n496\t// keeping it explicit means future changes to the attach side can't\n497\t// silently desync from the detach side.\n498\tfunc (s *Service) detachFromAllEnvs(ctx context.Context, project, service, secretName string) error {\n499\t\tenvs, err := s.envsForService(ctx, project, service)\n500\t\tif err != nil {\n501\t\t\treturn err\n502\t\t}\n503\t\tns := s.nsFor(ctx, project)\n504\t\tfor _, e := range envs {\n505\t\t\tif e.Spec.Kind == \"preview\" {\n506\t\t\t\tcontinue\n507\t\t\t}\n508\t\t\tnext := make([]string, 0, len(e.Spec.EnvFromSecrets))\n509\t\t\tfor _, existing := range e.Spec.EnvFromSecrets {\n510\t\t\t\tif existing != secretName {\n511\t\t\t\t\tnext = append(next, existing)\n512\t\t\t\t}\n513\t\t\t}\n514\t\t\tif len(next) == len(e.Spec.EnvFromSecrets) {"}]},"uuid":"fa451ab4-8e28-4596-9fb1-49388c0a556f","timestamp":"2026-07-24T14:23:31.403Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"server-go/internal/secrets/secrets.go","content":"// boot with no inherited config so reviewers see whether the change\n// works against a fresh slate, and so production secrets never leak\n// into a throwaway URL. Excludes <addon>-conn and shared secrets.\n130\tfunc isManagedServiceSecretName(name, project, svcShort string) bool {\n131\t\tprefix := project + \"-\" + svcShort + \"-\"\n132\t\treturn len(name) > len(prefix) &&"}]},"uuid":"58070934-3ce5-474f-a132-4e00d118b5df","timestamp":"2026-07-24T14:24:09.597Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"server-go/internal/projects/managed_secret_env.go","content":"// managedSecretNamesFor returns the kuso-managed service-secrets entries\n// present in envFromSecrets (the <svc>-secrets / <svc>-<env>-secrets ones),\n// NOT the <addon>-conn entries (addon-owned, already shown as secretKeyRefs).\nfunc managedSecretNamesFor(envFromSecrets []string, project, svcShort, envKind, envName string) []string {\n\twant := map[string]bool{\n\t\tkube.ServiceSecretName(project, svcShort): true,\n\t}\n\t// Env-scoped secret name uses the env's short name; the env CR name is\n\t// <project>-<service>-<envshort>, so recompute against the trailing part.\n\tfor _, s := range envFromSecrets {\n\t\tif want[s] {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Accept any <project>-<svc>-*-secrets entry (env-scoped managed secret).\n\t\tif isManagedServiceSecretName(s, project, svcShort) {\n\t\t\twant[s] = true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tout := make([]string, 0, len(want))\n\tfor _, s := range envFromSecrets {\n\t\tif want[s] {\n\t\t\tout = append(out, s)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// The bare <svc>-secrets may not be in envFromSecrets but still exist;\n\t// include it so a service-level secret is always considered.\n\tbase := kube.ServiceSecretName(project, svcShort)\n\tfound := false\n\tfor _, s := range out {\n\t\tif s == base {\n\t\t\tfound = true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif !found {\n\t\tout = append(out, base)\n\t}\n\treturn out\n}\n\n// isManagedServiceSecretName reports whether name is a kuso-managed\n// service-secrets secret for (project, svcShort): <project>-<svc>-secrets or\n// <project>-<svc>-<env>-secrets. {"parentUuid":"58070934-3ce5-474f-a132-4e00d118b5df","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a3e05c7c400899d02","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdM2y3Q3ptKwMLLjvxwVD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"`managedSecretNamesFor` actually ignores the `envKind` parameter entirely (it matches by prefix pattern, not by kind) — so `managed_secret_env.go:79` passing `env.Spec.Kind` is a dead/harmless argument, not a bug. Good.\n\nI now have a complete picture. Here is the full classified audit.\n\n---\n\n# KusoEnvironment `spec.kind` Reader Audit — Overloaded-Field Bug\n\n## Root-cause recap (confirmed)\n\n`env_groups.go:605` stamps `Kind: \"production\"` on env-group CLONE members (staging/custom), with the comment `chart-side semantics: \"always-on env\"`. The env's REAL group is the `kuso.sislelabs.com/env` label + the `kuso.sislelabs.com/env-group-kind` annotation/label. So `spec.kind==\"production\"` means \"always-on / not-a-preview\", NOT \"belongs to the production env-group\".\n\n## Operator / Helm consumption (Task item 3) — decisive\n\n- `operator/helm-charts/kusoenvironment/templates/_helpers.tpl:15` — `.Values.kind` is used ONLY to stamp the label `kuso.sislelabs.com/env-kind`. It does not drive TTL/scaledown/always-on in any template.\n- `ingress.yaml`, `hpa.yaml`, `values.yaml` — no branching on `kind` for always-on/TTL. The `Stopped` page is driven by `spec.stopped`, HPA by the resolved `replicaCount`/`autoscaling` block.\n- Always-on / scale-to-zero is decided server-side in `activator.go:450` (`Spec.Kind != \"production\"`), and TTL/GC in `envs_ops.go:79` (`Spec.Kind == \"preview\"` + `Spec.TTL`).\n- `operator/config/crd/bases/...kusoenvironments.yaml:28` — `spec.kind` is only a printer-column jsonPath.\n\n**Conclusion:** The chart/operator does NOT differentiate \"production\" from \"custom\" for runtime behavior — only \"preview\" vs not. Setting a clone's `spec.kind` to `\"custom\"` would keep it out of `Kind==\"preview\"` teardown/scale-down paths, so **always-on/TTL behavior would be preserved**. HOWEVER, several server-side chart-semantics readers use `!= \"production\"` (activator) or `== \"production\"` rather than `== \"preview\"`, so a clone-side change from \"production\" is NOT safe without also touching those. **Recommended fix direction: canvas/reader-side (leave `spec.kind` alone; buggy readers should switch to the `kuso.sislelabs.com/env` label).**\n\n---\n\n## Classified readers\n\n### BUGGY-GROUPING (reads `spec.kind==\"production\"` to mean \"canonical prod node / belongs to prod group\")\n\n| file:line | condition | why buggy | suggested fix |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `web/src/components/canvas/ProjectCanvas.tsx:172` | `allForService.find((e) => e.spec.kind === \"production\")` | Picks the service's \"canonical\" node env; a staging clone also has kind==\"production\", so `.find` may return the staging clone (the exact tickero symptom in the comment). | Prefer env where `metadata.labels[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"] === \"production\"`, fallback to `spec.kind===\"production\"`, then `[0]`. |\n| `web/src/components/canvas/ProjectCanvas.tsx:783` | `x.spec.service === ... && x.spec.kind === \"production\"` | Same — context-menu \"open prod URL\" can resolve to a staging clone's URL. | Match `labels[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"] === \"production\"`. |\n| `web/src/app/(app)/projects/page.tsx:290` | `if (e.spec?.kind !== \"production\" \\|\\| !e.spec?.host) continue;` | Builds prod-host-for-service; a staging clone (kind==\"production\", has host) can win when the service-label cross-check at :293 doesn't uniquely disambiguate (esp. the \"lone\" fallback at :297). | Filter on `labels[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"] === \"production\"`. |\n| `web/src/app/(app)/projects/page.tsx:486` | `e.spec.service === fqn && e.spec.kind === \"production\"` | \"liveServices\" count reads a service's prod env status; may read a staging clone's readiness → wrong LIVE count. | Match env label == \"production\". |\n| `web/src/app/(app)/projects/page.tsx:528` | `e.spec.service === s.metadata.name && e.spec.kind === \"production\"` | Same for the public-URL \"live\" pick. | Match env label == \"production\". |\n| `web/src/components/service/overlay/ServiceLogsPanel.tsx:44` | `if (e.spec.kind === \"production\") return { value: \"production\", label: \"production\" }` | A staging clone gets mapped to the dropdown value/label \"production\", colliding with the real prod option (and hiding \"staging\"). | Use `labels[\"kuso.sislelabs.com/env\"]` as the source of the option value; only the env whose label==\"production\" should render as \"production\". |\n| `web/src/components/service/ServiceOverlay.tsx:427` | `if (envParam === \"production\") return e.spec.kind === \"production\";` | When the URL selects env-group \"production\", this matches ANY kind==\"production\" env incl. a staging clone → overlay can bind to the wrong env. The very next lines (:428-429) already use the correct `kuso.sislelabs.com/env` label for non-prod. | Change the production branch to `envLabel === \"production\"` (label already read at :428), keeping the legacy last-segment fallback. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/services_ops.go:1647` | `if envs[i].Spec.Kind != \"production\" { continue }` then `prodEnvByService[Spec.Service] = env` | Builds \"the service's production env\" map for auto-domain Host/TLS resolution (`buildServiceResolver`). A staging clone (kind==\"production\") can overwrite the real prod entry (map keyed by service FQN; last-write-wins). | Select by `Labels[kube.LabelEnv] == \"production\"`. |\n| `server-go/internal/builds/cards.go:294` | `if e.Spec.Kind != \"production\" { continue }` (then uses `e.Spec.Host` as the service's public link) | Falls back to \"the production env host\"; a staging clone can be chosen, yielding staging.<domain> as the service card link. | Select by `Labels[kube.LabelEnv] == \"production\"`. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/projects_ops.go:527` | `envKind := e.Spec.Kind; if envKind==\"\" { envKind = e.Labels[LabelEnv] }` then `EnvSecretName(name, short, envKind)` | On project delete, computes the env-secret name from `Spec.Kind`. For a staging clone Spec.Kind==\"production\" → deletes/targets `...-production-secrets` instead of `...-staging-secrets`, leaving the real per-env secret orphaned. (Best-effort delete, so not fatal, but wrong name.) | Prefer `e.Labels[kube.LabelEnv]` first, fall back to Spec.Kind. |\n\n### AMBIGUOUS (grouping-ish, needs human decision — behavior partly correct today)\n\n| file:line | condition | note |\n|---|---|---|\n| `server-go/internal/http/handlers/kubernetes_env_metrics.go:175` | `if envs[i].Spec.Kind == \"production\"` | Comment (:141) says the intent is \"don't let previews jitter the card\" — that's CHART semantics (exclude ephemerals), and `!= \"preview\"` would satisfy it. BUT it currently ALSO sums staging clones (kind==\"production\") into the project-card CPU/mem, which arguably over-counts a non-prod group. If the card is meant to be \"production env-group only\", this is BUGGY-GROUPING (use label==\"production\"). If it's meant to be \"all always-on envs\", it's CORRECT-CHART but the `==\"production\"` should be `!=\"preview\"` for clarity. Human decision on desired card scope. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/envs_ops.go:137` | `if e.Spec.Kind == \"production\" { return \"cannot delete production environment\" }` | Blocks `DeleteEnvironment` on prod. A staging clone has kind==\"production\", so it's ALSO undeletable via this path — likely unintended (you should be able to tear down a staging clone). Probably should guard on `Labels[LabelEnv]==\"production\"` (the true prod group). But env-group clones may be deleted via a different code path (`env_groups` deletion) — verify before changing. Leaning BUGGY-GROUPING. |\n| `server-go/internal/secrets/secrets.go:568` | `if e.Name == env \\|\\| e.Spec.Kind == env \\|\\| strings.HasSuffix(e.Name, \"-\"+env)` | `findEnv` env-name resolver. `Spec.Kind == env` matches when caller passes `env=\"production\"`; a staging clone (kind==\"production\") would ALSO match `env=\"production\"`, so `findEnv(...,\"production\")` could return the staging clone. But the `HasSuffix(e.Name,\"-\"+env)` clause typically disambiguates for named envs. Minor mis-resolution risk for the bare \"production\" lookup. Consider matching `Labels[LabelEnv]==env` instead of `Spec.Kind==env`. |\n\n### CORRECT-CHART (legitimately \"is this always-on / not-a-preview\" — `spec.kind` is the right field)\n\n| file:line | condition | why correct |\n|---|---|---|\n| `server-go/internal/activator/activator.go:450` | `Spec.Kind != \"\" && Spec.Kind != \"production\" && !Spec.Stopped` | Scale-to-zero routing: previews GC on TTL, always-on envs (incl. clones) route for WAKE. Clones being treated as always-on here is intended. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/envs_ops.go:79` | `Spec.Kind != \"preview\" \\|\\| TTL==nil ...` | TTL sweep only reaps previews. Correct chart semantics. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/drift.go:317` | `if env.Spec.Kind != \"preview\"` | Drift compares scale only on non-ephemeral (non-preview) envs. Correct. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/propagate.go:261` | `if changed.Scale && env.Spec.Kind != \"preview\"` | Don't bleed HPA into pinned-1-replica previews. Correct. |\n| `server-go/internal/secrets/secrets.go:470` | `if e.Spec.Kind == \"preview\" { continue }` (attachToAllEnvs) | Shared secrets skip ephemeral previews. Correct. |\n| `server-go/internal/secrets/secrets.go:505` | `if e.Spec.Kind == \"preview\" { continue }` (detachFromAllEnvs) | Symmetric with attach. Correct. |\n| `server-go/internal/projects/services_ops.go:1190` | `if oldEnv.Spec.Kind == \"preview\" { continue }` (rename) | Rename skips ephemeral previews. Correct. |\n| `web/src/components/service/overlay/ServiceDeploymentsPanel.tsx:133` | `if (env?.spec?.kind === \"production\")` → confirm redeploy | Intent is \"this is an always-on/live env, confirm before rebuilding\".
